MAT	<p>316c_3f9c</p> <p>Match each of the following hypothesis tests with their corresponding descriptions.</p><p><i>Note:</i> Each choice will be used exactly once.</p>	<strong><span style="color: #003fff;">Two-Sample F-test</span></strong>	Used to test whether two different batches of products have equal <strong><span style="color: #076dad;">variability</span></strong> in their measurements.	<strong><span style="color: #d40000;">Benchmark Z-test</span></strong>	In a <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> of bacteria, the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">average</span></strong> gene expression level for a key metabolic gene is 100 units, with a <strong><span style="color: #007576;">standard deviation</span></strong> of 6 units. You collect n = 40 treated bacterial <strong><span style="color: #007a00;">samples</span></strong> and want to test if their gene expression level differs from the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>.	<strong><span style="color: #6c6c00;">Benchmark T-test</span></strong>	The <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> protein concentration is 50 mg/mL, but the <strong><span style="color: #007576;">standard deviation</span></strong> is <strong><span style="color: #a719db;">unknown</span></strong>. You are working with n = 20 <strong><span style="color: #007a00;">samples</span></strong> and want to test if the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> protein concentration differs from the known <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>.	<strong><span style="color: #c80085;">Analysis of Variance (ANOVA)</span></strong>	You are studying bird <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong>s across five different habitats. You collect n = 25 bird counts from each habitat and want to test if there is a significant difference in the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> number of birds observed across the habitats.	<strong><span style="color: #00775f;">Two-Sample T-test</span></strong>	You are comparing the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> gene expression of two cell lines, one treated and one untreated, with n = 25 <strong><span style="color: #007a00;">samples</span></strong> in each group. You want to test if the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>s are <strong><span style="color: #0067cc;">significantly different</span></strong>.
MAT	<p>316c_fd5b</p> <p>Match each of the following hypothesis tests with their corresponding descriptions.</p><p><i>Note:</i> Each choice will be used exactly once.</p>	<strong><span style="color: #c80085;">Analysis of Variance (ANOVA)</span></strong>	You want to compare the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> number of plant species across five habitats, with n = 20 plots in each habitat. You want to test if any habitat shows a <strong><span style="color: #0067cc;">significantly different</span></strong> <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>.	<strong><span style="color: #d40000;">Benchmark Z-test</span></strong>	Compares the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> of a sample to a known <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> when the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> variance is known.	<strong><span style="color: #00775f;">Two-Sample T-test</span></strong>	You want to compare the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">average</span></strong> soil pH between two agricultural fields, one using organic fertilizer and one using conventional methods, with n = 30 soil <strong><span style="color: #007a00;">samples</span></strong> from each field. You want to test if the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>s are <strong><span style="color: #0067cc;">significantly different</span></strong>.	<strong><span style="color: #6c6c00;">Benchmark T-test</span></strong>	Used when the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> variance is <strong><span style="color: #a719db;">unknown</span></strong>, and you have a smaller <strong><span style="color: #b74300;">sample size</span></strong> (n &lt; 30).	<strong><span style="color: #003fff;">Two-Sample F-test</span></strong>	You are comparing the variance of protein concentrations between two experimental batches, each with n = 25 <strong><span style="color: #007a00;">samples</span></strong>, to see if their <strong><span style="color: #076dad;">variability</span></strong> differs.

You collect n = 25 bird counts from each habitat and want to test if there is a significant difference in the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> number of birds observed across the habitats.	<strong><span style="color: #6c6c00;">Benchmark T-test</span></strong>	The <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">average</span></strong> blood sugar level in the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> is 90 mg/dL, but the <strong><span style="color: #007576;">standard deviation</span></strong> is <strong><span style="color: #a719db;">unknown</span></strong>. You collect a sample of n = 20 patients and want to test if their <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">average</span></strong> blood sugar level differs from the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>.	<strong><span style="color: #00775f;">Two-Sample T-test</span></strong>	You are comparing the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">average</span></strong> enzyme activity between two experimental conditions, one treated with a new inhibitor and one with a control, with n = 20 <strong><span style="color: #007a00;">samples</span></strong> in each group. You want to test if the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>s are different.	<strong><span style="color: #003fff;">Two-Sample F-test</span></strong>	Used to test whether two different batches of products have equal <strong><span style="color: #076dad;">variability</span></strong> in their measurements.

You collect n = 25 bird counts from each habitat and want to test if there is a significant difference in the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> number of birds observed across the habitats.	<strong><span style="color: #00775f;">Two-Sample T-test</span></strong>	Compares the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>s of two independent groups to determine if they are statistically different from each other.	<strong><span style="color: #003fff;">Two-Sample F-test</span></strong>	You are comparing the <strong><span style="color: #076dad;">variability</span></strong> in protein yield between two different laboratory protocols, each using n = 25 <strong><span style="color: #007a00;">samples</span></strong>. You want to test if the <strong><span style="color: #cc0066;">variances</span></strong> in protein yield are <strong><span style="color: #0067cc;">significantly different</span></strong>.	<strong><span style="color: #c80085;">Analysis of Variance (ANOVA)</span></strong>	Compares the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>s of more than two groups to determine if at least one group's <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> is <strong><span style="color: #0067cc;">significantly different</span></strong> from the others.	<strong><span style="color: #d40000;">Benchmark Z-test</span></strong>	Compares the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> of a sample to a known <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> when the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> variance is known.	<strong><span style="color: #6c6c00;">Benchmark T-test</span></strong>	Used when the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> variance is <strong><span style="color: #a719db;">unknown</span></strong>, and you have a smaller <strong><span style="color: #b74300;">sample size</span></strong> (n &lt; 30).

You collect a sample of n = 25 patients on a new drug to test if their <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> heart rate differs from the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>.	<strong><span style="color: #00775f;">Two-Sample T-test</span></strong>	You want to test if the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> number of species differs between two regions, one pristine and one affected by deforestation, with n = 25 plots in each region.	<strong><span style="color: #d40000;">Benchmark Z-test</span></strong>	The <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> pH in a wetland is 7.5 with a <strong><span style="color: #007576;">standard deviation</span></strong> of 0.32. You are sampling n = 50 water <strong><span style="color: #007a00;">samples</span></strong> to check if the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">average</span></strong> pH has changed over time.	<strong><span style="color: #003fff;">Two-Sample F-test</span></strong>	You are comparing the variance of protein concentrations between two experimental batches, each with n = 25 <strong><span style="color: #007a00;">samples</span></strong>, to see if their <strong><span style="color: #076dad;">variability</span></strong> differs.

You collect a sample of n = 20 patients and want to test if their <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">average</span></strong> blood sugar level differs from the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>.	<strong><span style="color: #003fff;">Two-Sample F-test</span></strong>	You are comparing the <strong><span style="color: #076dad;">variability</span></strong> in blood pressure between two groups, one with n = 25 patients on a standard drug and another with n = 40 patients on an experimental drug. You want to test if the <strong><span style="color: #cc0066;">variances</span></strong> are different.

You are testing a sample of n = 100 patients to see if the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> blood pressure differs from 120 mmHg.	<strong><span style="color: #c80085;">Analysis of Variance (ANOVA)</span></strong>	You are testing if the <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong> DNA replication rate differs across four different temperatures, with n = 20 <strong><span style="color: #007a00;">samples</span></strong> per temperature. You want to test if any group shows <strong><span style="color: #0067cc;">significantly different</span></strong> replication rates.	<strong><span style="color: #6c6c00;">Benchmark T-test</span></strong>	Used when the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> variance is <strong><span style="color: #a719db;">unknown</span></strong>, and you have a smaller <strong><span style="color: #b74300;">sample size</span></strong> (n &lt; 30).

You collect n = 40 treated bacterial <strong><span style="color: #007a00;">samples</span></strong> and want to test if their gene expression level differs from the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> <strong><span style="color: #935d00;">mean</span></strong>.	<strong><span style="color: #6c6c00;">Benchmark T-test</span></strong>	Used when the <strong><span style="color: #3b7600;">population</span></strong> variance is <strong><span style="color: #a719db;">unknown</span></strong>, and you have a smaller <strong><span style="color: #b74300;">sample size</span></strong> (n &lt; 30).	<strong><span style="color: #003fff;">Two-Sample F-test</span></strong>	Compares the <strong><span style="color: #cc0066;">variances</span></strong> of two independent groups to test if the <strong><span style="color: #076dad;">variability</span></strong> between them is <strong><span style="color: #0067cc;">significantly different</span></strong>.
